# Abilities

Generated by `Tools/SdbDocs` from a build-1962 `clientdb.sd2` (db patch prod-1962, built 2016-05-05).

`dbitems::AbilityModule` holds 30941 rows and 6724 of them have no English name.
A module is the item you slot; the ability it grants is a chain in `apt::AbilityData`, and several
hundred modules can point at one chain. Grouping by chain gives **2446 abilities**
(10 chains referenced by a named module do not exist in `apt::AbilityData` and are left out).

`Stats` is the item card: each entry is an attribute from `dbitems::AttributeRange` with its base
value across the ability's modules. Where a range appears, that is the spread from the weakest
module to the strongest, which is the ability's whole progression in one cell -- `Base Damage
300 to 13322.8` is one ability at level 1 and at level 50. Attribute names come from
[Attributes](Attributes.md), display name where the client has one and internal name otherwise, and
the internal names are frequently more informative.

`Requires` is the certificate gating the modules, which is usually the battleframe. It is the only
frame attribution that survives: `dbitems::Battleframe` points at an ability group id whose table is
not in the client db, and table names are stored as hashes so it cannot be found by guessing.

Descriptions are the client's own strings and still carry its markup tokens.

## Abilities with a card

284 abilities whose modules carry stats or name a certificate -- everything a player
could read an item card for. Ordered by certificate so a frame's kit reads together, with the ones
that name no certificate after them.

## The rest

The other 2162. No stats and no certificate, which is the shape of a visual effect hook, a
token grant, an NPC behaviour or a designer's test rig -- they share the table with real abilities
and are not distinguishable from them by any flag, only by having nothing to show. Listed rather
than dropped, because deciding by guesswork which of these was never player-facing is how a wiki
ends up asserting what the data does not say.
